### Q536: What is the normal ankle brachial index (ABI)?

- **A)** More than 1
- **B)** Less than 0. 8
- **C)** 0. 9
- **D)** 1

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The ABI is calculated by measuring the blood pressure in both upper limbs using the highest systolic BP. The ankle BP is measured by placing the cuff above the ankle and measuring the return of flow in the dorsalis pedis by a Doppler probe. The ratio of both ankle and brachial is called ABI and is normally higher than 1.

### Q537: Which artery supplies collateral flow between the celiac artery (CA) and superior mesenteric artery (SMA)?

- **A)** Middle colic artery
- **B)** Pancreaticoduodenal arteries
- **C)** Left colic artery
- **D)** Marginal artery of Drummond
- **E)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The superior pancreaticoduodenal artery is a branch of the gastroduodenal, which in turn is a branch of the hepatic branch of the celiac trunk. The inferior pancreaticoduodenal artery is a branch of the superior mesenteric artery. Both pancreaticoduodenal arteries anastomose with each other as they supply the head of pancreas and the duodenum.

**Explanation:** The marginal artery of Drummond runs along the whole length of the colon. It connects the SMA with the IMA.

### Q541: What is the percentage of stenosis that increases the incidence of stroke in patients suffering from internal carotid artery (ICA) atherosclerosis?

### Q544: — Which is true regarding endovascular abdominal aortic aneurysm repair?

- **A)** It costs the same as open repair
- **B)** Requires lifelong follow-up imaging
- **C)** Results in longer hospital stay than open repair
- **D)** Requires more blood transfusion than open repair

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Endovascular repair requires lifelong follow-up. Endovascular repair is less invasive and less blood transfusion is needed, and the ICU stay and ordinary hospital stays are less than the open surgical repair. The main disadvantage of the endovascular procedure is its high cost. Endovascular repair cost is nearly twice as much as the open surgical repair.

### Q545: What is the incidence of coronary artery disease in patients with peripheral arterial disease?

- **A)** 10%,
- **B)** 60%
- **C)** 40%
- **D)** 70%

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Inevaluation of a patient with peripheral arterial disease, coronary and carotid disease should be investigated. About 40% of all patients with peripheral arterial disease have significant coronary artery disease (CAD) that might not be symptomatic due to the limited exercise ability. Significant CAD and carotid artery disease should have the priority in management. Myocardial infarction accounts for the majority of both early and late postoperative deaths if not noticed and properly treated.

### Q549: Aortography is indicated for

### Q550: S The following statements about abdominal aortic aneurysms (AAA) arc all true, except

### Q551: About abdominal aortic ancurysm (AAA), all the following statements are true, except

- **A)** Normal diameter of the abdominal aorta is 2-3cm
- **B)** Aortic aneurysms with a diameter of Sem are advised to have urgent repair
- **C)** The main risk of abdominal aortic aneurysm is rupture
- **D)** One of its complications is distal embolization
- **E)** CT scan is an excellent method of diagnosing abdominal aortic ancurysms

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The main risk is rupture. Formation of a thrombus in the aneurysm and distal embolization are possible other complications. ¢ The normal diameter of abdominal aorta is 2-3 cm. AAA is considered if diameter is greater than 4. 5-5 cm. This is considered a small AAA with an estimated annual risk of rupture of 1% and this risk is estimated to be 6-10% in 5 years. e For AAA with a diameter of 6-7 cm the estimated annual risk of rupture is 5-10% and 5-year risk of rupture is 50%. AAA with a diameter larger than 7 cm should be advised to have rapid elective intervention after assessment of the coronary and carotid artery status, as the annual rupture risk rate is about 15% and the 5-year risk of rupture is almost 100%.

### Q552: About Buerger's disease, all the following statements are true, except

- **B)** It affects male only
- **C)** It affects smokers only
- **D)** Pain is an early symptom
- **E)** Arterial reconstruction is not possible

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a type of arteritis that affects distal arteries of the lower limbs. It actually affects the neighbouring vein and nerve as well. Nerve inflammation is the cause of early occurrence and severity of pain. Arterial reconstruction is not feasible. Abstinence from smoking is essential to prevent progression of the disease. Lumbar sympathectomy can improve skin blood supply only.

### Q555: About arterial embolism, all the following statements are true, except

### Q556: Carotid atherosclerosis most often produces transient ischemic attacks or stroke by which of the following mechanisms?

- **A)** Reduction of flow to the affected arca of the brain because of arterial stenosis
- **B)** Embolization of atheromatous debris and/or clot with occlusion of intracranial branches of the carotid artery
- **C)** Thrombosis and propagation of the clot into the intracranial branches
- **D)** All of the above are equally common
- **E)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Approximately 70% of cases produced by carotid atherosclerosis are embolic. The surface of an atherosclerotic lesion at the carotid bifurcation is rough and invites formation of thrombi. Similarly, the atheromatous plaque can degenerate and rupture into the lumen, thus embolizing the intracranial branches of the internal carotid artery.

---

### Q557: About intermittent claudication, all the following statements are true, except

- **A)** ls elicited by exercise
- **B)** Subsides rapidly with rest
- **C)** Is often worse at night
- **D)** Does not indicate critical ischaemia
- **E)** May be an indication for bypass surgery

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Intermittent claudication is painful cramps that is characteristically elicited by a certain amount of exercise, and is rapidly relieved by cessation of that exercise. It is usually felt in a defined muscle group such as the calf muscles. Rest pain is felt in the toes and forefoot as opposed to a muscle group, and often begins at night. It indicates severe ischaemia and impending gangrene. Claudication, when sufficiently disabling, may be an indication for revascularization, particularly if it interferes with gaining a living.

---

### Q558: About arterial injuries one statement is true.

- **A)** All arterial injuries are associated with absence of palpable distal pulse
- **B)** Preoperative arteriography is always required to diagnose an arterial injury
- **C)** A supracondylar fracture of the femur commonly injures the superficial femoral artery
- **D)** Patients with critical limb ischemia have paralysis and paraesthesias
- **E)** In all patients with multiple trauma, arterial injuries should be repaired before

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Arteriography is not required for the diagnosis of most arterial injuries. It is, however, required to diagnose partial injury, a condition where distal pulse can be felt. A supracondylar femoral fracture tends to injure the popliteal artery. Patients who do not have adequate collateral develop critical ischemia, which manifests by paralysis and paresthesia. In patients with multiple injuries, priority is given to ensuring airway patency, effective breathing and control of external bleeding by compression.

---

### Q559: A 21-year-old woman presents with fingers’ color changes in response to cold stimulation. The become pale, cyanosed, then red. All the following statements are true, except

- **A)** The most probable diagnosis is Raynaud's disease
- **B)** Differential diagnosis includes cervical rib
- **C)** Differential diagnosis includes scleroderma
- **D)** Thoracic sympathectomy gives immediate good results
- **E)** The disease is commonly unilateral affecting the dominant hand

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The history favours Raynaud's disease. Cervical rib and scleroderma produce similar colour changes that are called Raynaud’s phenomenon. Thoracic sympathectomy gives immediate good results, though symptoms later recur but with less severity. The disease is bilateral and affects young females.

---

### Q560: About mesenteric ischaemia due to atherosclerosis, all the following statements are true, except

- **A)** Patients complain of postprandial abdominal pain
- **B)** Duplex is a useful diagnostic test
- **C)** Arteriography is necessary when surgery is planned
- **D)** Revascularization is indicated only if infarction occurs
- **E)** Intestinal infarction presents by intestinal obstruction and bleeding per rectum

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Like lower limb ischaemia, duplex is a useful diagnostic tool, but is not a substitute for arteriography when planning for revascularization. Because of the very poor prognosis of the extensive intestinal infarction in these cases, revascularization is done for patients with postprandial pain before gangrene sets in.

### Q561: Where are the Cockett perforators present?

- **B)** Medial lower leg

- **D)** Femoral triangle
- **E)** None of the above

**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The Cockett perforator veins drain the medial lower leg connecting the posterior arch vein (superficial) to the posterior tibial vein. In general, the perforator veins pierce the deep fascia to connect the superficial venous system with the deep venous system.

### Q562: What is the mechanism of action of heparin?

- **A)** Increase of the activity of antithrombin
- **B)** Increase the conversion of factor X to Xa
- **C)** Increase conversion of factor IX to 1Xa
- **D)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Heparin binds to antithrombin increasing its activity to over 900 folds. The antithrombin-heparin complex inhibits thrombin (IIa) and factor Xa. Heparin also binds to tissue factor inhibiting the conversion of factor X to Xa and IX to IXa.

### Q576: What is the treatment of symptomatic varicose vein of great saphenous vein tributaries with reflux at the saphenofemoral junction?

Sclerotherapy is contraindicated in this place because of the risk of escape of the sclerosant material to the deep system and the difficulty of maintaining compression for an adequate time. e Getting rid of the varicose tributaries. This is achieved either by sclerotherapy or by excision. If available, endovenous ablation is currently the treatment of choice, either by radiofrequency or by laser.

### Q585: Superficial thrombophlebitis can be associated with any of the following, except

### Q586: About stripping of the long saphenous vein, all the following statements are true, except

- **B)** It can cause injury of the saphenous vein
- **C)** It can cause injury of the sural nerve

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Commonly the tributaries, rather than the main trunk of the long saphenous, are the veins that become varicose. If the main vein is not dilated, there is no need to remove it. Stripping the long saphenous vein exposes the saphenous nerve to possible injury, but not the sural nerve which is related to the short saphenous vein. The long saphenous vein is the main source of venous grafts for femoropopliteal bypass and for coronary bypass operations.

### Q587: A $2-year-old woman complains of aching in the right lower leg for the past nine months, which is relieved by Icg elevation. She is also frequently awakened by night due to calf and foot cramping. On examination there are scattered superficial varicositics and leg edema. What is the most probable cause?

- **A)** Incompetent saphenofemoral valve
- **B)** Superficial lymphatic obstruction
- **C)** Arterial insufficiency
- **D)** Incompetent sapheno-popliteal valve
- **E)** Deep venous insufficiency

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Deep venous insufficiency is usually a sequel to deep vein thrombosis. That is why it is called post-phlebitic limb. The attack of DVT very frequently passes undiagnosed, and the patient truthfully denies any such history. The most common features of venous insufficiency are leg aching, oedema, and night cramps that often occur after periods of long inactive standing. Leg elevation frequently provides relief of symptoms due to venous insufficiency, while it increases pain due to arterial insufficiency. Other manifestations include scattered secondary varicose veins, as well as eczema, pigmentation and chronic ulcers of the lower leg.

---

### Q593: — Lymphocele following renal transplantation is best treated by

- **A)** Observation
- **B)** Aspiration
- **C)** Laparoscopic peritoncal window creation
- **D)** Open exploration and dealing according to findings

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Lymphocele is collection of lymph resulting from cut lymphatic vessels of the recipient. It can occur in up to 15% of all recipient patients. Clinically, it appears as a mass compressing the nearby structures for example ureter, iliac vein, and allograft artery. The patient may develop leg swelling on the side of the transplant with hypertension and elevation of serum creatinine. Ultrasonography reveals the diagnosis. The ideal method of treatment is creation of a peritoneal window to allow the fluid to drain into the peritoneal cavity. This could be done laparoscopically or by an open approach.

### Q595: What is the common vascular complication following liver transplantation?

**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Hepatic artery thrombosis has an incidence of thrombosis of about 5% in post liver transplant patients. The incidence in children is higher (about 10%). Doppler ultrasound is the initial method for diagnosis. Once the diagnosis is established, re-exploration and revision of the anastomosis using interrupted sutures is performed. If liver necrosis has occurred, re-transplantation is indicated.

**Explanation:** Epstein-Barr virus is a common cause of post-transplant lymphoproliferative disease. It is a member of the herpes virus family. ### Q598: About preoperative cross-matching between donor and recipient, all the following are true, except

- **B)** ABO compatibility is essential for survival of renal transplantations
- **C)** HLA compatibility is cssential for survival of liver transplantation
- **D)** HLA compatibility is essential for survival of renal transplantation
- **E)** HLA compatibility is essential for survival of bone marrow transplantation

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** ABO blood grouping compatibility is essential for all transplants. The aim is to prevent hyperacute rejection. HLA cross-matching to ensure compatibility is essential for renal and bone marrow transplantation, and is also important for pancreas transplantation. Histocompatibility provides a better chance of longterm graft survival. The test, however, does not affect the prognosis of liver transplantations.

### Q600: About graft rejection, one statement only is true

- **A)** Treating accelerated acute rejection with increasing immunosuppressive dose is usually successful
- **B)** Acute rejection is mediated by humoral immune mechanism
- **C)** Acute rejection can be treated with raising the dose of immunosuppressive therapy
- **D)** Acute rejection occurs within the first weck of transplantation
- **E)** Chronic rejection is successfully treated with raising the dose of

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Accelerated acute rejection occurs within the first week, usually within 3-4 days. Its treatment is graft removal. Acute rejection occurs within a month of the transplant and is mediated by a cellular immune mechanism. Treatment is by giving a high dose of steroids and OKTS3. Prognosis is good. Chronic rejection occurs after several months and is an irreversible process that requires re-transplantation.

### Q604: Regarding liver transplantation, all the following statements are true, except

- **A)** For adults, liver cirrhosis is the commonest disease for which liver transplantation is performed
- **B)** Two transplants can be provided from a single donor liver
- **C)** The liver is transplanted in an orthotopic position
- **D)** The donor may be cadaver or living related
- **E)** Liver transplantation is indicated in patients with advanced hepatocellular carcinoma

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** End-stage liver failure is the indication for liver transplantation. In adults, its commonest cause is liver cirrhosis. The commonest indication in childhood is biliary atresia. It is possible to transplant one half of a liver, either the right or the left lobe, because each has its separate arterial, portal, systemic venous and biliary systems. A cadaver liver, therefore, can be used for two recipients. Also, living-related transplantation is possible. The donor can live normally with a half liver. Furthermore, the remaining healthy liver has a remarkable ability for regeneration, thus adding a substantial amount of hepatic cells. Transplantation for primary liver cancer has a lower survival rate because immunosuppression leads to early recurrence. Patients with advanced disease are not candidates for transplantation. Early HCC cases, however, can sometimes be transplanted, but the selection of such patients is subjected to very strict criteria.

### Q610: What is the treatment of choice of small cell lung carcinoma?

**Explanation:** Small cell lung carcinoma is generally not treated by surgery. It accounts for about 15-20% of all lung cancers. It is an aggressive form of cancer that metastasizes early. ### Q613: About Pancoast's tumour, all the following statements are true, EXCEPT:

### Q614: A trauma victim has dyspnoea and a large flail segment of his chest wall due to multiple rib fractures. All the following statements are true, except

### Q615: ] All the following are indications for thoracotomy in a trauma victim, except

- **A)** Chest tube drains 400ml fresh blood at insertion in the pleural cavity
- **B)** Fresh blood drainage that continues at a rate more than 200ml/ hour
- **C)** Persistent air leak from the chest tube
- **D)** Associated oesophageal injury
- **E)** Forcign body in the chest

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** 400ml of blood at initial tube insertion is not an indication for surgery. What is more important is persistent bleeding. Most bleeding stops spontaneously when the lung inflates. This is because the common source is small pulmonary vessels that have low pressure. Persistent bleeding comes from either the internal mammary vessels or from the aorta and its branches. Persistent air leak indicates injury of a big branch of the bronchial tree, which requires repair.

### Q627: The differential diagnosis of postoperative pulmonary embolism includes all the following, except

### Q628: About the investigations for suspected pulmonary thrombembolism, all the following statements are true, except

### Q629: About postoperative atelectasis, all the following are true, except

- **E)** [f neglected may cause pneumonia

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Atelectasis usually presents by fever and tachycardia within the first few days after surgery. In extensive cases the patient may develop dyspnoea. If not timely treated, atelectasis leads to pneumonia. Thoracic and upper abdominal incisions cause pain on deep breathing and coughing. Patients tend to take shallow breathing, and are therefore more likely to develop atelectasis than with other incisions. Atelectasis does not present by hemoptysis.

### Q631: Which one is true for Stanford classification of aortic dissection?

- **B)** Type A - starts in the ascending aorta

Dissection spares the ascending aorta and the tear starts in the descending aorta, usually just distal to the origin of the left subclavian artery.

### Q632: What is the most common symptom in patients with acute type A aortic dissection?

- **A)** Dyspnoca
- **B)** Severe chest pain
- **C)** Syncope
- **D)** Palpitations

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Chest pain radiating to the back is the most common symptom in type A aortic dissection (90%). A high index of suspicion is important especially in younger patients or patients suspected of having connective tissue disorder. Pain is typically severe and occurs suddenly radiating to the back and abdomen. It is sharp and migrates distally with progression of the dissection.

### Q633: What is the most sensitive test used in the diagnosis of acute aortic dissection?

- **A)** Chest x-ray
- **B)** Cardiac enzymes
- **C)** dimer
- **D)** ECG

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** D-dimer is a very sensitive test for detection of aortic dissection (about 98%). ECG and elevated cardiac enzymes may also be of help as dissection could involve the coronary ostia and induce myocardial ischaemia and infarction. Chest x-ray may show widening of mediastinum, and this could alert the surgeon that dissection is a possibility.

### Q634: Which of the following is a complication of type A aortic dissection?

- **A)** Aortic valve incompetence
- **B)** Myocardial infarction
- **C)** Pericardial effusion and tamponade
- **D)** All of the above
- **E)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** All of the above are complications of type A aortic dissection. Aortic incompetence is due to commissural disruption. Myocardial infarction is due to extension of dissection into the coronary ostia especially the right coronary ostium, causing inferior wall myocardial infarction. Serosanguinous pericardial effusion occurs through the thin outer wall of the dissected aorta. Rupture of the ascending aorta is a fatal event.

### Q642: A hard mass in the testis of a 30-year-old man is best diagnosed by:

- **A)** Core needle biopsy
- **B)** Fine-needle aspiration cytology
- **C)** Orchiectomy
- **D)** Open biopsy and frozen section
- **E)** All of the above are equally safe and diagnostically accurate

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Percutaneous biopsy in testicular masses is contraindicated due to the high risk of seeding to the scrotal skin and consequent metastasis to inguinal nodes. Most hard testicular masses are malignant, so high orchiectomy is the best way of diagnosis and treatment. The operation is done through an inguinal incision. High orchidectomy means excision of the testis and the spermatic cord up to the internal inguinal ring.

### Q643: In patients with Fournier's gangrene, the testicles are

- **A)** Usually also affected
- **B)** Should be resected, as the testicles will eventually get gangrenous
- **C)** Are usually not involved
- **D)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Fournier’s gangrene is necrotizing fasciitis of the scrotum and perineum, usually in immunocompromised persons. Necrosis mostly affects the subcutaneous tissue, and the skin to a lesser extent. The testicles have separate blood supply (testicular artery from the aorta) and are not affected. Debridement by excision of all necrotic tissue under an umbrella of broad-spectrum antibiotics should be performed.

- **B)** Inability to reduce the foreskin after it has been retracted

**Explanation:** This is a condition in which the foreskin has been retracted, and the patient is unable to reduce it back causing constriction of the glans penis. The results are penile pain and oedema of the glans. If condition is not treated early, glans necrosis may occur.

### Q645: The patients with benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPI 1) are initially best treated by

- **B)** Beta stimulants
- **C)** Alpha blockers
- **D)** Alpha stimulants

**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Alpha blockers are the best initial medical treatment for BPH. They act on alpha receptors in the prostatic smooth muscle, thus decreasing its tone. Another group of drugs is the 5-alpha reductase inhibitors that inhibit production of dihydrotestosterone. They shrink the prostate over a few months, thus improving symptoms of BPH.

### Q649: Patients with hypernephroma (renal cell carcinoma) with IVC tumour thrombus are best treated by

- **A)** Chemotherapy
- **B)** Radiotherapy
- **C)** Chemotherapy and radiotherapy
- **D)** Open surgery

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** In about 5-10% of hypernephroma patients, the tumour invades the lumen of the renal vein and the IVC. The tumour thrombus is usually not adherent to the vein wall. Venotomy and extraction of the thrombus after clamping the IVC above and below the thrombus is performed. If the thrombus extends high up to the right atrium, cardiopulmonary bypass should be used and total circulatory arrest is performed to achieve complete removal in a bloodless field.

### Q653: About intravenous urography (IV), all the following statements are true, except

- **A)** It is not suitable for patients who are in renal failure
- **B)** It provides morphological and functional information about the kidney
- **C)** On anormal IVU the ureters pass down on the transverse processes of lumbar vertebrae 2-5
- **D)** It is the imaging of choice for diagnosis of suspected renal cell carcinoma
- **E)** Emergency 1VU can be done for patients in acute renal colic,

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** IVU provides morphological information about the kidneys, ureters and bladder such as strictures, dilatations and filling defects, It also provides a rough idea about the separate functions of each kidney. In a normal kidney a renal blush (contrast that is filtered in the cortical tubules) shows within one minute of IV injection of the contrast. The calyces and renal pelvis start to appear within five minutes and the ureters start to show at 10-13 minutes of injection. In a kidney with impaired function these intervals are prolonged. The delay is roughly proportionate to the degree of renal functional impairment. CT is far better than IVU in the diagnosis and staging of hypernephroma. Emergency IVU is useful in the diagnosis of patients with renal colic, though this is now superseded with spiral CT scan (without contrast), which is better able to detect small stones. Another alternative is abdominal ultrasound+ plain X-ray.

### Q656: Five hours after being hit by a car, a 32-year-old woman presents to the emergency room complaining of left flank pain and gross hacmaturia, she is hacmodynamically stable. The next step in management is

- **A)** Exploratory laparotomy
- **B)** Diagnostic peritoncal lavage
- **C)** Intravenous urography
- **D)** Triple contrast CT scan of the abdomen
- **E)** Blood transfusion

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Abdominal trauma victims are divided into categories: * Haemodynamically unstable or those with frank peritonitis. These should have emergency resuscitation and laparotomy. Haemodynamically stable with local abdominal symptoms and signs that are suggestive of an internal injury. These should be admitted to hospital under observation and are subjected to diagnostic peritoneal lavage, abdominal ultrasound or triple contrast CT (oral, 1V and rectal contrasts). CT is the best. Haemodynamically stable with no local abdominal manifestations. These are discharged home, provided there are no injuries elsewhere. In this case there is suspicion of left renal injury. Diagnostic peritoneal lavage (DPL) is not suitable as the haematoma is usually restricted to the retroperitoneal space. DPL can detect only intraperitoneal blood and intestinal content. The majority of isolated renal injuries do not require surgery. Haematuria tends to stop spontaneously because of the tamponade effect of the posterior peritoneum. Admission to hospital and follow up for vital signs, local signs, haematocrit and urine colour are recommended. Blood transfusion is given when there is evidence of major blood loss. In the described case it is not indicated.

---

### Q659: About testicular malignancies, one statement only is true.

**Explanation:** In prevalence, lung and prostate cancers far exceed those of the testis in males. Alfa fetoprotein is elevated with teratomas rather than seminomas. Lymphatic spread is directed upwards towards the para-aortic lymph nodes. Spread to the inguinal nodes may happen if the tumour invades the scrotal skin, which is rare. Trans-scrotal biopsy is contraindicated as it opens the way to scrotal skin invasion.

### Q660: The commonest symptom of renal cell carcinoma is

- **A)** Renal colic
- **B)** Varicocele
- **C)** Abdominal mass
- **D)** Jaundice
- **E)** Haematuria

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Haematuria is present in 60% of cases. It is classically described as being painless, recurrent, profuse and total. Pain is the next common symptom. It may be due to stretch of the renal capsule by the neoplasm, passage of blood clots causing ureteric colic or by infiltration of adjacent lumbar nerves causing referred pain. A renal mass is the least frequently seen of the triad “haematuria, pain and mass". Secondary varicocele is a rare complication of renal cell carcinoma. It is caused by occlusion of the testicular vein by tumour growth in the inferior vena cava or in the left renal vein.

### Q661: About incomplete descent of the testis, only one statement is true.

**Year:** None

**Explanation:** One third of cases is bilateral. The commonest position is at the neck of scrotum, while the least common is the intra-abdominal testis. Orchidopexy should be done between the ages of 6-12 months. Early surgery preserves the spermatogenic function of the testis but does not obviate the risk of malignant transformation. The difficulty in mobilizing the testis down to the scrotum usually arises from a short testicular artery.

### Q665: A 14-year-old boy presents with acute pain and swelling of the scrotum for the last two hours. On examination, the right side of the scrotum is swollen and tender. Urgent duplex scan shows no flow in the testicular vessels. The next step is

- **A)** Urgent surgery on the right compartment of the scrotum
- **B)** Urgent surgery on both sides of the scrotum
- **C)** Fine needle aspiration cytology
- **D)** Intravenous antibiotics
- **E)** Non-steroidal anti-inflammatory agents

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is the classic picture of testicular torsion. Treatment is urgent surgery because every hour counts. Delay is likely to result in gangrene of the testis. The best results are obtained if surgery is done within six hours of the start of torsion. If Duplex ultrasound is readily available it would be valuable in confirming the diagnosis, provided it wouldn't delay surgical intervention. The right side of the scrotum is explored. If the testis is viable it is untwisted and is then fixed in the scrotum with nonabsorbable sutures to prevent recurrent torsion. This is called orchidopexy. If the testis is gangrenous, orchidectomy is done. Left orchidopexy should also be done because the predisposing factor to torsion may be bilateral.

### Q675: A 72-ycar-old woman fel! on outstretched arm. This was followed by pain and by swelling above the wrist. Plain X-ray showed fracture of the distal radius. About this fracture, all the following statements are true, except

- **A)** This is called Colles’ fracture
- **B)** There is anterior displacement and angulation of distal fragment
- **C)** There is radial displacement and angulation of distal fragment
- **D)** Osteoporosis is the usual predisposing factor
- **E)** The majority of these cases are treated by closed reduction, and

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Breaking a fall with an outstretched arm fractures the lower radius. The distal fragment is displaced and angulated posteriorly. This is called Colles’ fracture. The resulting deformity is likened to a dinner fork. There are also radial deviation and angulation; and impaction of the distal fragment. Falling on a flexed wrist is less frequent and causes fracture in the same place but with anterior displacement of the distal fragment. This is called Smith's fracture. Osteoporosis is usually present in Colles’ fracture patients. The majority are treated conservatively. Open reduction and internal fixation are indicated in a few cases where closed reduction of a significant deformity is not successful.

### Q677: Which one of the following fractures is known to cause bone necrosis?

### Q679: ] About complications of fractures, all the following statements are true, except

- **A)** Fracture of the shaft of humerus can cause wrist drop
- **B)** Fracture of the medial epicondyle of humerus can cause claw hand deformity
- **C)** Fracture of the surgical neck of humerus can cause atrophy of deltoid muscle
- **D)** Extracapsular fracture of neck of femur can cause necrosis of its head
- **E)** Supracondylar fracture of the femur can cause popliteal artery injury

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** fracture of the shaft of humerus can injure the radial nerve as it lies posteriorly in the spiral groove. This causes wrist drop due to paralysis of wrist extensors. The ulnar nerve is prone to injury with fractures of the medial epicondyle. This produces clawing of the medial two fingers as the corresponding lumbrical muscles are paralysed. The circumflex (axillary) nerve passes posterior to the surgical neck of humerus. Its fracture can cause nerve injury with consequent paralysis and atrophy of the deltoid muscle. The capsule of hip joint is an important landmark in the prognosis of femoral neck fractures. An extracapsular fracture does not affect the vascularity of the neck. An intracapsular fracture, however, can interrupt its blood supply and cause head necrosis if it is markedly displaced. Therefore, for necrosis of head of femur to occur, the fracture should be intracapsular and displaced. Supracondylar fractures of the femur can injure the popliteal artery and cause acute ischaemia and blood loss.

---

### Q680: Internal fixation is indicated for all the following fractures, except

- **A)** Extracapsular fracture of neck of femur
- **B)** Displaced ankle fractures
- **C)** Supracondylar fracture of the femur with popliteal artery injury
- **D)** Fracture of the shaft of femur in an adult
- **E)** Compound fracture of the tibia

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Femoral neck fractures usually affect elderly people who have osteoporosis. Internal fixation allows early mobilization and reduces the risks of venous thrombosis, chest infection and decubitus ulcer formation. Ankle fractures are indicated for open reduction and internal fixation in order to avoid any irregularity of the articular surface, a condition that causes osteoarthritis and chronic pain in this weight-bearing joint. As a general rule arterial repair requires rigid fixation of the fracture that caused it. Otherwise, the vascular repair is threatened by failure. The femoral shaft is surrounded by strong muscles that can re-displace the fracture if it is fixed in plaster. Traction requires lying down in a hospital bed for a long time. Internal metal fixation solves these problems. Internal fixation is generally contraindicated in cases of compound fractures. These fractures are contaminated. The insertion of a metal prosthesis in such a wound promotes infection. If rigid immobilization is required, external metal fixation is a suitable alternative.

### Q685: About osteosarcoma, one statement only is true.

### Q686: About osteosarcoma, all the following statements are true, except

- **A)** Plain X-ray shows Codman’s triangle
- **B)** Definitive diagnosis rests on a combination of clinical and radiographic findings
- **C)** Wide excision is the main treatment of early cases
- **D)** Limb salvage is possible in early cases
- **E)** Chemotherapy improves outcome of treatment

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Codman’s triangle is subperiosteal bone deposition that is formed when the periosteum is raised by the tumor. For the treatment of early cases wide excision with a safety margin, in all directions, is indicated. This may amount, in some cases, to amputation of the limb. However, in small tumours, limb salvage (preservation) by inserting a prosthesis in place of the excised bone, is possible. Before doing this major, and possibly mutilating, surgery, adequate bone biopsy should be obtained to confirm the diagnosis. The addition of chemotherapy improves the outcome. The trend is to give it before the operation (neoadjuvant chemotherapy). A combination of methotrexate, adriamycin and cisplatin is used.

### Q687: About giant cell tumours of bone, which one statement is true?

- **A)** It usually arises from the shaft of a long bone
- **B)** It affects elderly people
- **C)** The tumour is always highly malignant
- **D)** It does not invade the articular cartilage of the nearby joint
- **E)** Radiographically, it shows both bone destruction and new bone formation

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Giant-cell tumours account for 4-5% of primary bone tumors. It usually arises from the epiphysis at the growing end of a long bone; though it may arise in other bones as the mandible. The usual sites are the lower end of femur, upper ends of tibia or fibula, upper end of humerus and lower ends of radius and ulna. The tumour affects young adults in the third and fourth decade. Giant cell tumours are usually benign though they may exist in a wide range of aggression. The malignant form is rare. A characteristic feature is that it respects the articular cartilage. The tumour is also sharply demarcated. On plain X-ray, it shows as an osteolytic area with sharp demarcation and no bone formation.

### Q703: About intracranial pressure (ICP), all the following arc true, except

**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`

Deteriorating level of consciousness is an indication of rising intracranial pressure. In a head-injury victim intracranial hypertension may be caused by: e Intracranial haematoma. This requires surgical evacuation and haemostasis. e Cerebral oedema. This is treated conservatively. Most important is to ensure adequate oxygenation, which may require mechanical ventilation. Diuretics are also helpful.

### Q706: In radial nerve crush injury, the most appropriate treatment is

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Closed peripheral nerve injuries should be observed and EMG should be done 4 weeks after injury. If conduction is improving, continuous observation is indicated. Surgical exploration and correction are indicated if there is no improvement after 12 weeks. Electrical testing during surgery should be done and if there is no conduction, end-to-end anastomosis should be performed. If the gap is big a nerve graft is needed.

### Q711: An expanding extradural haematoma causes all the following, except

- **A)** Hypertension and bradycardia
- **B)** Anaemia
- **C)** Deteriorating level of consciousness
- **D)** Vomiting
- **E)** Ipsilateral pupillary dilatation and contralateral hemiplegia

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** An intracranial haematoma never induces anaemia. The blood loss is limited by the hard confines of the skull. An expanding haematoma within the skull causes two sets of manifestations Manifestations that are caused by rise of intracranial tension. These include gradual deterioration of consciousness, vomiting, bradycardia and hypertension. Lateralizing manifestations. In the case of an extradural haematoma, these manifestations include weakness on the opposite side, and paralysis of the pupil on the same side.

### Q712: A 72-year-old woman bumped her head while climbing in a car. Two weeks later she has headache, lack of concentration and sleeps for unusually long hours. The most probable diagnosis among the following is

- **A)** Extradural hacmatoma
- **B)** Subdural haematoma
- **C)** Intracerebral hacmatoma
- **D)** Intraventricular bleeding
- **E)** Subaracnoid haemorrhage

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is the classic description of a chronic subdural haematoma; an elderly person suffers minor head trauma and a few weeks later develop these symptoms. With advancement of age the brain atrophies a little. With minor trauma it moves within the skull and tearing small veins. As venous pressure is smaller than the arterial one, a haematoma accumulates gradually in the subdural space. As it liquefies, its osmotic pressure attracts water and further expands, still at a slow rate.

### Q719: Patients with factor V Leiden mutation are more liable to develop DVT than normal population. What is the reason?

- **B)** Inability to inactivate factor V

- **D)** None of the above

**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Patients with factor V Leiden mutation have a variant of factor V that the body is unable to inactivate. They are more liable to develop DVT especially in long-hour flights. Homozygous-mutation patients should be anticoagulated for life.

### Q737: Regarding hypokalaemia, all the following statements are true, except

- **A)** Serum potassium of 2. 5 mmol/L is Hypokalacmia
- **B)** Hypokalaemia causes acidosis
- **C)** Severe diarrhoea may cause Hypokalaemia
- **D)** Hypokalaemia causes muscle weakness
- **E)** Hypokalaemia causes paralytic ileus

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The normal serum potassium is 3. 5 to 5 mmol/L. When serum potassium level drops, potassium ions (K+) move out of cells to the extracellular space in an attempt to normalize its serum level. This occurs in exchange for another positively charged ion, which is hydrogen (H+). The latter moves from the extrato the intra-cellular compartment. The reduction of H+ in the extracellular fluid causes alkalosis.

---

### Q738: ] About metabolic acidosis, one statement only is true.

- **A)** It is caused by pyloric stenosis
- **B)** It causes hyperkalacmia
- **C)** Arterial bicarbonate level is elevated
- **D)** pH is above 7. 4
- **E)** Respiratory rate is reduced

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Pyloric stenosis causes loss of hydrochloric acid. This results in metabolic alkalosis. Hydrogen ion goes in the cells in exchange for potassium that goes out, causing hyperkalaemia. Arterial bicarbonate level is reduced. Acidosis means pH below 7. The patient hyperventilates to wash out carbon dioxide.

### Q744: All the following nerves are blocked by epidural anaesthesia except

These three kinds of nerves are blocked as they exit from the spinal cord. There is no effect on the parasympathetic nervous system.

### Q752: The most likely anaesthetic to induce hepatitis is

Hepatitis that is caused by the first halothane exposure is usually mild and may pass un-noticed, but a second exposure is commonly fatal.

### Q753: A child has a painless swelling at the outer angle of the eye that has been present since birth. Which statement about this lesion is true?

- **A)** It is a sebaceous cyst
- **B)** It may extend intracranially
- **C)** It is precancerous
- **D)** Surgical excision carries a risk of injury to vision
- **E)** This lesion occurs only at this site

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a typical appearance of an external angular dermoid cyst. It is caused by subcutaneous inclusion of portions of the surface epithelium along the lines of fusion of cutaneous dermatomes during feta) life. Although present since birth, they may not appear clinically except after few years when the cyst begins to distend. They usually occur at lines of dermatome fusion, as in this example at the outer angle of the eye, at the root of the nose, around the ear or along the midline of the body. Sometimes an external angular dermoid has a dumbbell configuration, whereby it extends intracranially through the skull. Sebaceous cysts do not appear in childhood.

---

### Q754: A tailor develops a small finger swelling after repeated minor punctures and trauma. What is the most likely diagnosis?

- **A)** Sebaceous cyst
- **B)** Osteochondroma
- **C)** Enchondroma
- **D)** Implantation dermoid cyst
- **E)** Simple ganglion

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Implantation dermoid cysts occur secondary to punctures, lacerations or incisions, which displace some epithelial cells into the subcutaneous tissues. The displaced cells form a dermoid cyst. These cysts occur mainly in the fingers, palm of the hand or sole of the foot. They are frequently seen in farmers, factory workers, carpenters and tailors who are subject to repeated minor traumas to the hand. If it bothers the patient, the only treatment is surgical excision. Sebaceous cysts do not develop in non-hairy skin because it is devoid of sebaceous glands such as the palmar surface of the hand and the plantar surface of the foot. Osteochondromas develop at the growing ends of long bones, particularly the lower femur. Enchondromas typically affect the phalanges, but cause circumferential swelling of bones. A simple ganglion is classically found on the back of the wrist region.

---

### Q755: A 3-year-old boy has a combined cleft lip and palate. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This is the optimal age for surgical repair
- **B)** Management requires a multidisciplinary team
- **C)** Otitis media is a possible complication
- **D)** Maternal smoking during pregnancy is a predisposing factor
- **E)** Cleft palate repair is generally performed before 18 months of age

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a combined cleft lip and palate. The lip defect is recommended for repair before the age of 6 months, while the palate surgery should be done before 18 months. For best results the services of a paediatric surgeon, paediatrician, dentist, and speech therapist are needed.

---

### Q756: A patient has Fournier gangrene. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** The lesion is caused by a polymicrobial infection
- **B)** The pathology is necrotizing fasciitis
- **C)** Diabetes is a predisposing factor
- **D)** HIV infection is a predisposing factor
- **E)** Urgent surgery is required to save the testes

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is Fournier’s gangrene, which is a form of necrotizing fasciitis of the skin and subcutaneous tissue that affects, but may not be limited to, the scrotum. Combined multiple aerobic and anaerobic bacteria are always incriminated. They gain access either through perianal infection, urethritis, or a breach of the skin. Immunosuppression, as with diabetes and HIV infection, is a strong predisposing factor. Gangrene is rapidly progressive and causes sepsis syndrome. Urgent surgery is required to control sepsis by excising dead tissues, but Not to save the testes as they are not affected by this infection.

---

### Q757: A patient has an ingrown toenail. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This is a painful condition
- **B)** Wearing tight shoes is a predisposing factor
- **C)** Faulty nail trimming is a predisposing factor
- **D)** Surgery is the only effective treatment
- **E)** Removal of the whole nail is not usually needed

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is an ingrown toenail, i. c. , a toenai] that has grown into the skin instead of over it. This usually happens fo the big toe. An ingrown toenail commonly gets infected and cause severe pain, swelling of nail fold and pus discharge. Tight shoes and cutting the nail too short or rounding its edge can cause it to grow into the skin. Early cases are treated conservatively by wedging a tiny cotton ball, under the corner of the ingrown nail to lift the nail off of the skin and maintaining local hygiene until the nail grows out and can be trimmed. Oral antibiotics are sometimes needed to resolve soft tissue infection. Advanced and persistent cases require surgery. The initial surgical approach is excision of the ingrown side of the nail. If it recurs, excision and/or destruction of the corresponding germinal matrix with phenol is added.

### Q758: A boy has a recurrent midline discharging sinus after excision of a thyroglossal duct cyst. All the following statements are correct except:

- **A)** The correct nature of this lesion is a fistula, not a sinus
- **B)** The discharging opening may move upward with deglutition
- **C)** The discharging opening may move upward with tongue protrusion
- **D)** Treatment involves excision of the tract toward the base of the tongue
- **E)** Recurrence may follow incomplete excision

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Though known as thyroglossal fistula, it is, in fact, a sinus as it ends blindly in the base of the tongue. This is never a de novo lesion. The actual congenital lesion is the thyroglossal cyst, which ruptures when infected or is incompletely removed. The opening typically moves up with tongue protrusion. Sistrunk operation is the only treatment that entails excision of the opening and the track that extends upwards to the tongue base and includes the central part of the hyoid bone.

### Q760: A child has a congenital hairy nevus. All the following statements are true except:

**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a congenital hairy nevus. Development of a melanoma occurs in up to 15% of such lesions. This is the main danger. Therefore, excision of the diseased skin is indicated, usually in stages (serial excision) as shown by the scars in this patient. Tissue expanders and skin grafting help cover the resulting defect. Radiotherapy has no role in the management of this condition.

### Q761: A patient develops a keloid in a burn scar. All the following statements are true except:

Additional measures are, therefore, used to reduce this incidence. These include local steroid injection, prolonged compression and radiotherapy.

### Q762: A painful dark collection develops beneath the nail after a crush injury to the fingertip. All the following statements are true except:

### Q763: A patient has a facial plexiform neurofibroma. One statement about this condition is true:

### Q764: A diabetic patient with neuropathy has a punched-out ulcer over the head of the first metatarsal. The ulcer is most likely:

- **A)** A neuropathic ulcer
- **B)** An ischaemic ulcer
- **C)** A venous ulcer
- **D)** Squamous cell carcinoma
- **E)** A tuberculous ulcer

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a neuropathic ulcer whose main cause is diabetic neuropathy. As the patient cannot perceive pain, he does not protect himself from repeated trauma. The commonest site is opposite the head of the first metatarsal bone, which is a weight-bearing point. The ulcer is characterized by having a punched-out edge and a callous margin. It may be deep enough to cause chronic osteomyelitis of the underlying bone.

### Q765: A patient develops chronic pain on standing, oedema, skin discoloration, secondary varicose veins, and a possible ulcer after deep vein thrombosis. One statement about this post-phlebitic limb is true:

- **A)** This condition is painless
- **B)** It is essentially caused by primary varicose veins
- **C)** Doppler ultrasound is needed for assessment
- **D)** The main line of treatment is surgical
- **E)** The saphenofemoral valve is always incompetent

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a post-phlebitic lower limb, i-e. , it follows deep vein thrombosis, which destroys the valves of the deep and the communicating veins. The condition is characterized by pain on prolonged standing, oedema (though not apparent here), dark colour of the lower third of the leg whose skin becomes stiff, scattered secondary varicose veins (here seen in the foot) and possibly a chronic ulcer (this is the typical location). Doppler (or duplex) study is needed to check for possible narrowing of the deep veins and to locate incompetent perforating veins. Treatment is essentially conservative by using elastic stockings, elevation and ulcer care. Surgery is seldom needed to ligate incompetent perforating veins.

### Q766: A boy has mild subcoronal hypospadias. One statement about this condition is true:

- **A)** Its incidence is one in 2, 000 male births
- **B)** This is a severe form of the disease
- **C)** The urinary stream may be abnormal
- **D)** Circumcision should precede surgical correction by at least two months
- **E)** The best time for surgical repair is at three years of age

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is hypospadias, a common congenital anomaly that affects one in 250-300 male births where the external urethral meatus is present on the under-surface of the penis. The photo shows a mild form of the disease as the external opening is in a sub-coronal position. Together with the glanular and coronal varieties they are termed anterior hypospadias. The posterior varieties are worse as the penis is curved downwards and the scrotum may even be bifid if the opening is in the perineum. As the external meatus is abnormally oriented, and may be narrowed, the child suffers spraying of urine stream. Circumcision should never precede surgical correction of the abnormality as the remaining prepuce skin (the hood) is made use of to reconstruct the missing distal urethra. The best time of repair is between four and 18 months. Delaying surgery exposes the child to psychological trauma.

---

### Q767: A slowly growing facial ulcer has a raised, rolled-in edge. What is the most likely diagnosis?

- **A)** Traumatic ulcer
- **B)** Basal cell carcinoma
- **C)** Squamous cell carcinoma
- **D)** Ulcerating melanoma
- **E)** Kaposi sarcoma

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is the typical position (face) and shape of basal cell carcinoma that is called rodent ulcer. The ulcer is described as having raised rolled-in edge. Many such lesions are pigmented. It does not necessarily indicate a melanoma. Remember also that some melanomas are non-pigmented (amelanotic). The three types of skin cancer, basal and squamous cell carcinomas as well as melanomas have a common predisposing factor, which is excessive exposure to ultraviolet light.

### Q768: A patient has basal cell carcinoma. Which of the following is not an appropriate treatment?

- **A)** Radiotherapy
- **B)** Topical chemotherapy cream
- **C)** Curettage and electrodessication
- **D)** Surgical excision with a safety margin
- **E)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is basal cell carcinoma (BCC), which is the mildest form of skin cancer. Squamous cell carcinoma (SCC) is worse while melanoma is the most aggressive. BCC is locally malignant, i. e. , it spreads locally but not by lymphatics or blood. Therefore, its eradication does not require removal of lymph nodes. Any of the above-mentioned methods, in addition to cryosurgery (freezing), can effectively cure it.

---

### Q769: A newborn has a compressible, translucent swelling in the posterior triangle of the neck. What is the main line of treatment?

- **A)** Surgical excision
- **B)** Aspiration
- **C)** Injection of a sclerosing agent
- **D)** Radiotherapy
- **E)** Chemotherapy

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is cystic hygroma, a congenital disorder of the lymphatics, and this is its commonest site. The swelling is compressible (doughy) and is usually translucent (as shown in the figure). Surgical excision is the mainstay of treatment; though complete eradication of the disease is not always attained because of proximity to important structures. Sclerotherapy is a less effective option. Aspiration is inadvisable except as an emergency measure in the rare condition of the swelling causing suffocation, to be followed by excision. Chemo and radiotherapy have no role, as this is a benign condition.

---

### Q770: A newborn has bladder exstrophy (ectopia vesicae). All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** There is wide separation of the pubic bones
- **B)** The red area below the umbilical cord is the mucosa of the posterior bladder wall
- **C)** The baby has severe hypospadias
- **D)** Recurrent pyelonephritis is the commonest cause of death
- **E)** The patient may develop squamous cell carcinoma

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Bladder exstrophy (ectopia vesicae) is a rare congenital anomaly where the Lower part of anterior abdominal wall and anterior wall of the bladder are deficient. The inner surface of posterior wall of the bladder bulges through the defect. Urine spurts onto the abdominal wall from the exposed ureteral orifices. The pubic bones are widely separated. An umbilical hernia is always present. In males, there is associated epispadias with a short widened penis. The commonly lethal complication is recurrent pyelonephritis. Prolonged exposure of the bladder mucosa may cause squamous metaplasia and later squamous cell carcinoma. Treatment is by staged surgical repair that starts by closure of the bladder and anterior abdominal wall early in the neonatal period. Wide separation of the pubic bones can be overcome by bilateral iliac osteotomies.

---

### Q771: A 19-year-old female who has recurrent painful ulcers of the oral cavity. They heal spontaneously within 7-10 days. One statement only is true.

- **B)** Smoking is a predisposing factor

- **D)** These are termed aphthous ulcers

**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Aphthous ulceration is a common oral cavity painful condition. Ulcers remain for 7-10 days and disappear spontaneously. Ulcers recur with variable frequency, which diminishes with age. Despite its high incidence the exact cause is not known and, similarly, there is no known definite preventive or treatment measure.

### Q772: A child has ankyloglossia (tongue tie). All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This is a congenital problem
- **B)** It may affect dentition
- **C)** It may affect breastfeeding
- **D)** It may affect speech
- **E)** Some patients require surgery

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Tongue tie (ankyloglossia) is a congenital condition that restricts the tongue's range of movement. A short frenulum tethers the bottom of the tongue's tip to the floor of the mouth. It varies in degree of severity. Severe restriction of tongue movement may affect the ability to suck for breast-feeding, and may later affect speech. It never affects teeth development. Severe cases may require surgical release of the tight frenulum.

### Q773: An invertogram in a neonate with a high anorectal anomaly shows bowel gas ending above the pelvic floor. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** The line from the coccyx to the pubic symphysis represents the pelvic floor
- **B)** This is always an isolated anomaly
- **C)** The baby may pass meconium per urethra
- **D)** Treatment usually requires a temporary colostomy
- **E)** Fecal continence may be impaired after definitive treatment

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is high anorectal anomaly where an invertogram shows that bowel gas does not cross the pelvic floor (dotted line between tip of coccyx and symphysis pubis). An anorectal anomaly may be associated with other anomalies that are summed up by the acronym VACTERL. This stands for Vertebral (spina bifida with its various types), Anorectal, Cardiac, Tracheal, Esophageal, Renal and Limb anomalies. With such a high anorectal anomaly there is likely to exist a fistula between the rectum and the bladder (or with the vagina in females). This child may, thus, pass meconium per urethra. Surgical correction is classically staged and includes a temporary colostomy. As anal sphincters are deficient, even after a pull through operation, continence is imperfect as it depends only on the function of the levator ani.

---

### Q774: A patient has a painful, red swelling over the olecranon after repeatedly leaning on the elbow. What is the most likely diagnosis?

### Q775: Two days after severe head trauma, a patient develops bruising over the mastoid process (Battle sign). Which statement is true?

- **C)** It indicates the presence of an extradural haematoma

It is commonly accompanied by cerebral injury, escape of CSF or blood through the nose or ear, and cranial nerve palsies.

### Q776: A 56-year-old woman has a progressively growing, irregular, multicoloured pigmented lesion with ulceration and satellite lesions on the left shoulder. What is the most likely diagnosis?

- **A)** Melanoma
- **B)** Squamous cell carcinoma
- **C)** Pigmented basal cell carcinoma
- **D)** Kaposi sarcoma
- **E)** None of the above

**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The pigmented elevated skin lesion is most probably a melanoma because of the following signs Irregular outline The lesion is multicoloured e Ulceration (on the right hand side of the photo) Satellite lesions (on the lower part of the photo) Melanoma is the most aggressive skin cancer. It spreads locally, by lymphatics and by blood. Prognosis depends, to a large extent, on its depth. The thicker the melanoma, the worse is the prognosis. Two other features are characteristic of melanoma. e It may recur many years after treatment. e In addition to the usual metastatic sites as the lungs, liver and brain, a melanoma may spread to some unusual sites as the adrenal glands, intestine and the myocardium.

### Q777: A bluish cystic swelling in the floor of the mouth is most likely:

### Q779: Regarding peau d'orange of the breast, all the following statements are true except:

### Q780: A patient has an opening next to the anal verge that intermittently produces a small amount of discharge. All the following statements are true, except:

- **A)** This condition is usually a complication of an abscess
- **B)** Another opening is expected to be present internally in the midline posteriorly
- **C)** In the majority of cases an internal opening is situated above the anorectal ring
- **D)** The discharge is usually caused by mixed aerobic and anaerobic bacteria
- **E)** Surgery is the main line of treatment

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The patient has an anal fistula. The majority of cases have a low internal opening, ie. , below the anorectal ring, actually at the dentate line. High fistulas are rare. As the external opening lies posterior to an imaginary line that horizontally bisects the anal verge, the internal opening is expected to be situated in the midline posteriorly. External openings that lie anterior to this line have their internal openings in a radially opposite position. Low anal fistulas are treated surgically either by fistulectomy (excision of the whole track between the two openings) or by fistulotomy (widely laying open the track and curettage). The wound is left open to heal by secondary intention.

### Q781: A patient has multiple painless cystic swellings of the scrotum. What is the most likely diagnosis?

- **A)** An encysted hydrocele of the spermatic cord
- **B)** A sebaceous cyst
- **C)** A subcutaneous lipoma
- **D)** A testicular tumour
- **E)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** B

**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The scrotum is one of the common sites for sebaceous cysts. Frequently such cysts are multiple (notice the other cyst at the bottom of the scrotum). A subcutaneous lipoma in the scrotum is practically unknown as the scrotum is devoid of subcutaneous fat.

### Q782: A facial flame burn victim that has just arrived at the emergency room. There are no other burns in his body. All the following statements are true, except

- **A)** Closed-space burns carry a worse prognosis
- **B)** Admission to ICU is advisable
- **C)** The patient may require urgent escharotomy
- **D)** The patient may require endotracheal intubation
- **E)** Pulmonary oedema is one of the

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Facial burns carry a high risk of inhaling flames and toxic fumes that damage the respiratory tract. The injury consequences are worse if the burn occurs in a closed space. Inhalation damages the upper airway causing laryngeal oedema and stridor and may injure the lungs causing pulmonary oedema and respiratory failure. Because of these dangerous consequences the patient is better admitted to ICU for the high possibility of needing intubation and mechanical ventilation. Escharotomy is the odd one out. It is actually indicated for deep circumferential burns of the trunk and limbs in order to release the constricting eschar.

### Q783: This 42-year-old man had an abdominoperineal resection of low rectal cancer. All the following statements are true, except

**Correct Answer:** A

**Explanation:** Abdominoperineal resection includes the anal canal with its sphincters. Therefore, there is no chance of restoring the normal defecation route. That is why this is a permanent left colostomy.

### Q784: A patient has familial adenomatous polyposis with adenomas confirmed on colonoscopy and biopsy. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** The disease usually presents with acute abdominal pain
- **B)** The disease is inherited as an autosomal dominant trait
- **C)** Patients may have osteomas and desmoid tumours
- **D)** The villous variety has a greater tendency to malignant transformation than the tubular variety
- **E)** Definitive treatment of this patient is surgical

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is Adenomatous Polyposis Coli (APC). The usual symptoms are mild bleeding per rectum, anaemia, diarrhea, and/or chronic abdominal pain. If neglected, the patient will certainly develop adenocarcinomas of the colon and rectum. The disease is inherited as an autosomal dominant trait. Gardner's syndrome is a variant of APC where, in addition to the intestinal polyps, patients develop osteomas particularly in the mandible and skull, cysts, soft tissue tumours, and desmoid tumours of the abdominal wall. The only treatment is removal of the whole colon and rectum as soon as possible in order to avoid the development of cancer. It is quite important to screen brothers, sisters, sons and daughters regularly by colonoscopy to pick up affected persons at an early stage.

### Q785: A patient has a neck swelling that moves with deglutition and dilated neck veins. The appearance most likely indicates:

- **A)** Graves disease
- **B)** Extension of a goitre
- **C)** Hashimoto thyroiditis
- **D)** Multiple endocrine neoplasia
- **E)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Extension of goitre from the neck down to the mediastinum causes compression of the great veins and appearance of such dilated collaterals over the sternum. It also commonly causes dyspnea and dysphagia because of tracheal and oesophageal compression. Retrosternal extension that causes such compression is an indication for thyroidectomy. The great majority of retrosternal goitres can be removed from a neck incision. It is rare to require a sternotomy. Thyroid enlargement in cases of Graves’ disease is usually mild, and may even be impalpable. Hashimoto’s goitre can cause the appearance of such dilated veins, only if it extends down into the mediastinum to become retrosternal.

### Q786: A patient develops a firm, freely mobile cervical swelling four months after total thyroidectomy for papillary thyroid cancer. What is the recommended first investigation?

- **A)** CT scan
- **B)** MRI
- **C)** Thyroid isotope scan
- **D)** Plain X-ray of the neck and chest
- **E)** Ultrasound with guided fine-needle aspiration

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Papillary thyroid cancer is known to spread mainly by lymphatics. The first possibility is metastasis to a lymph node. Ultrasound is the imaging study of first choice for thyroid diseases. If combined with guided fine needle aspiration cytology (FNA) it often settles the diagnosis. Almost the same accuracy of detecting and taking biopsies from lymph nodes is obtained with CT or MRI, but these tests are more expensive and may not be available in Small peripheral hospitals. Isotope scan is a second step if cytology proves malignancy. It serves looking for deposits in the rest of the body.

### Q787: Two months after left hemicolectomy, a patient develops a reducible lower abdominal wall swelling that decreases when lying down. Which is not a cause of this lesion?

- **A)** Wound infection
- **B)** Chronic obstructive pulmonary disease
- **C)** Ascites
- **D)** Inappropriate closure of the peritoneum
- **E)** Hypoalbuminaemia

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is an incisional hernia. Peritoneal closure does not add to wound strength. It is, in fact, sound closure of the linea alba that counts. All other choices can cause disruption of linea alba closure. In this case wound infection (better called surgical site infection SSI) is the most likely cause because the broad scar indicates healing by secondary intention.

### Q788: A swelling lies in the midline just above the umbilicus and becomes more prominent when standing. What is the most likely diagnosis?

- **A)** Subcutaneous lipoma
- **B)** Soft tissue sarcoma
- **C)** Infantile (true) umbilical hernia
- **D)** Paraumbilical hernia
- **E)** Desmoid tumour

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is the typical position and appearance of a paraumbilical hernia, known also as adult type of umbilical hernia. The defect is in the linea alba, i. e. , in the midline, usually just above the umbilicus and less commonly below it. As the hernia enlarges it tends to compress the umbilical scar that takes this crescent-like appearance. On the other hand, an infantile type of umbilical hernia (also called true umbilical hernia) has its defect just deep to the umbilicus scar. As it enlarges the umbilical scar itself bulges forwards. The infantile type affects infants where it usually resolves spontaneously within two to three years. When an adult develops an infantile type hernia, always suspect ascites.

---

### Q789: An elderly man has bilateral direct inguinal hernias, one of which is recurrent. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** The patient has bilateral direct inguinal hernias
- **B)** Benign prostatic enlargement may be an underlying cause
- **C)** Age-related muscle weakness may contribute
- **D)** Repairing this hernia requires placement of mesh
- **E)** Laparoscopic repair is contraindicated

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The patient has bilateral direct inguinal hernias. The right one is recurrent, as shown by the overlying inguinal scar. Direct inguinal hernias appear in elderly males, are commonly bilateral, do not descend to the scrotum, have a hemispherical shape, and reduce spontaneously on lying down. B & C are frequent causes. Hernioplasty with a mesh is required to strengthen the weak abdominal musculature. Laparoscopic repair is quite suitable, particularly with bilateral and/or recurrent cases. A large mesh is placed behind the anterior abdominal muscles, in the preperitoneal space to cover the direct inguinal defect as well as the potential defects for oblique inguinal and femoral hernias.

---

### Q790: A man has a right inguinoscrotal swelling. What is the most likely diagnosis?

### Q795: A 64-year-old woman develops oedema of the left arm three years after treatment for left breast cancer. Which of the following may be a cause?

- **D)** Cancer recurrence in the axilla
- **E)** All of the above

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Oecedema of the limb after mastectomy may be lymphatic or venous. Exaggeration of the wrist crease points at a lymphatic nature. All are probable causes. A combination of factors may be present. Patients are advised to take utmost care to avoid injuries and infections of the ipsilateral limb. Injections and placements of IV cannulas on this side are contraindicated. Lymphangitis that may accompany limb infections tend to obstruct the remaining few patent arm lymphatics and exaggerate oedema.

### Q796: A 16-month-old boy has a_ scrotal swelling that appears during the day. At night, it resolves. On examination, there is a fluctuant swelling in the left side of the scrotum. It is not possible to get above the swelling. No bowel sounds are auscultated on the swelling. The most probable diagnosis is

- **A)** Congenital hydrocele
- **B)** Oblique inguinal hernia
- **C)** Epididymal cyst
- **D)** Varicocele
- **E)** Testicular teratoma

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a congenital hydrocele where there a patent processus vaginalis with a tiny communication between the peritoneum and the tunica vaginalis. It is too small to allow passage of bowel or omentum to form a hernia. The opening, however, allows the passage of peritoneal fluid that fills the tunica during the day and returns to the abdomen when he lies down by night. Transillumination confirms that the tunica is filled with clear fluid. As the tunica is prolonged up to the peritoneum, it takes an inguinoscrotal distribution when it is full. This is in contrast to adult vaginal hydrocele, which forms a completely scrotal swelling, i. e. , the examiner's fingers can get above the swelling.

### Q797: A patient has acute paronychia with pus beneath the cuticle. All the following statements are true except:
