- **B)** The causative organism is usually Staphylococcus aureus
- **C)** Diabetes mellitus is a predisposing factor
- **D)** Pus drainage can be done without anaesthesia
- **E)** Antibiotics are complementary to surgical drainage

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is acute paronychia. Pulp space infection is anterior. It is, in fact, deep infection and the tough overlying skin does not allow pus to be seen. Here pus is seen under the cuticle. Its drainage is performed by cutting through the insensitive cuticle with a scalpel or scissors. Pus drainage is the main line of treatment. Antibiotics may be added to speed up resolution of the surrounding cellulitis.

### Q799: An 18-year-old woman has a lateral neck swelling with a central opening that discharges a small amount of fluid. What is the most likely pathology?

- **A)** Branchial fistula
- **B)** Thyroglossal fistula
- **C)** Tuberculous sinus
- **D)** Hodgkin lymphoma
- **E)** Pilonidal sinus

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a case of tuberculous lymphadenitis with a sinus formation. This is also the site of a branchial cyst, but such a cyst does not form a sinus. A branchial fistula is not accompanied with a swelling and is situated in the lower neck at the anterior border of the sternomastoid muscle. A thyroglossal fistula is a midline opening in the front of the neck. This is also the site of a Hodgkin's disease swelling, yet it does not form a sinus. Pilonidal sinus occurs in the natal cleft, and rarely in the umbilicus. The treatment of a TB sinus is mainly by antituberculous medications. Repeated aspiration is done if it contains tuberculous pus. Excision of the mass is seldom needed.

### Q801: A child has a right parotid swelling and facial nerve paralysis. What is the most likely diagnosis?

### Q802: A patient has Klippel–Trenaunay syndrome. All the following statements are true except:

**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is Klippel Trenaunay syndrome, a congenital condition that is characterized by multiple arteriovenous fistulas, limb gigantism and secondary varicose veins. A hyperdynamic circulation can cause heart failure.

### Q803: $2 32-year-old male, who is a chronic smoker, complains of pain in the forefoot at rest. Choose the one correct statement.

- **A)** The disease commonly involves the hands
- **B)** Iliac and femoral arteries are expected to be patent
- **C)** Sympathectomy relieves foot pain
- **D)** The same pathology affects the coronary arteries
- **E)** The same pathology affects the renal arteries

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The relatively young age (below 40), male gender, smoking, rest pain and loss of toes point at the diagnosis of thromboangiitis obliterans (Buerger’s disease). The disease affects the distal lower limb arteries and their accompanying veins and nerves and causes rest pain. Sympathectomy improves skin circulation but not the rest pain.

### Q804: A 60-year-old woman has chronic right lower-limb ischaemia with dry gangrene. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This is dry gangrene
- **B)** Rest pain is expected and worsens on elevation
- **C)** Treatment is urgent amputation of the toes
- **D)** Arteriography is indicated
- **E)** The underlying pathology may involve the coronary arteries

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is dry gangrene that is most likely caused by atherosclerosis of the lower limb arteries. The disease can affect other arteries in the body. Of particular importance are the coronaries and the carotids, which can endanger life of the patient if major surgery is required to restore vascularity of the limb. The patient is expected to have rest pain because ischaemia is severe enough to cause gangrene. Arteriography is indicated to map out the arterial tree obstructions and patencies in order to decide feasibility and type of revascularization, which is the only hope to save (salvage) the limb. Removal of gangrenous toes is not urgent. In addition, it will not relieve rest pain nor obviate the threat of more extensive gangrene.

---

### Q805: A child has extreme sensitivity to sunlight with multiple freckle-like skin lesions. What is the most likely diagnosis?

- **A)** Xeroderma pigmentosum
- **B)** Multiple melanomas
- **C)** Innocent freckles
- **D)** Solar keratosis
- **E)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is the classic appearance of xeroderma pigmentosum. Xeroderma pigmentosum is a rare autosomal recessive genetic disease. The skin is highly sensitive to sunlight, which causes the development of basal and squamous cell carcinomas. In this patient, the ulcer on the side of the nose turned out to be a basal cell carcinoma. The affected person is advised to totally avoid exposure to sunlight and to use topical sunscreen agents.

### Q806: A 17-year-old front-seat passenger in a car accident has periorbital bruising. Which statement is correct?

- **A)** The tube is used for feeding
- **B)** Periorbital bruising may indicate an anterior cranial fossa fracture
- **C)** Skin lacerations should be sutured within a few hours to reduce scarring
- **D)** Ultrasound is the best method for detecting skeletal injury
- **E)** None of the previous statements is true

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Fracture of the anterior cranial fossa causes this panda-eye appearance, subconjunctival haemorrhage, CSF rhinorrhea and cranial nerve injuries. This is an endotracheal tube that has been placed to secure airway. It is used in the following trauma situations Obstructed airway. Threatened obstruction, eg. with bleeding or edema of the upper aerodigestive tract, particularly in comatose patients. ¢ To allow mechanical ventilation in cases of respiratory failure, eg. , massive lung contusion and flail chest. In this case the tube was introduced through the nose, rather than the mouth, probably due to difficulty related to jaw fractures. Suturing skin lacerations can be postponed for more than 24 hours in order to attend to more serious injuries. This does not affect the cosmetic result. The best method to assess facial bone fractures is CT.

---

### Q807: A 54-year-old farmer has a steadily growing scalp lesion with an everted edge. Which statement is true?

- **A)** The shape of the edge suggests an epithelioma (squamous cell carcinoma)
- **B)** Invasion of the skull is an indication for radiotherapy
- **C)** Submandibular lymph nodes are expected to be enlarged
- **D)** Bloodstream spread is common with this lesion
- **E)** Adjuvant chemotherapy is indicated

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The everted edge, in addition to the history, favours the diagnosis of squamous cell carcinoma (epithelioma). The main line of treatment is surgical excision with a safety margin and excision of involved lymph nodes. Though radiotherapy is also effective, invasion of bone is a contraindication. Squamous cell carcinoma rarely spreads by blood. It spreads by lymphatics to lymph nodes. In this case it may spread to the occipital nodes. Submandibular lymph nodes do not drain the scalp. Chemotherapy has no role in the treatment of squamous cell carcinoma of the scalp.

---

### Q808: A trauma patient is asked to hold a sheet of paper firmly between the thumb and index finger by adducting the thumb. He flexes the thumb to maintain the grip. This finding points to:

### Q809: A neonate has an omphalocele containing bowel and liver within a hernia sac. What is the diagnosis?

Gastroschisis is another birth defect of the abdominal wall. The baby's intestines, without a sac, protrude outside of the baby's abdomen, exiting through a defect to the right the umbilicus. Paraumbilical hernia is a disease of adults.

### Q810: A patient has chronic unilateral left lower-limb oedema with thickened skin and changes of lymphoedema. What is the most likely cause?

- **A)** Hypoalbuminaemia
- **B)** Post-phlebitic limb
- **C)** Congenital arteriovenous malformation
- **D)** Lymphatic oedema
- **E)** Soft tissue sarcoma

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Lymphatic oedema is the most probable cause. Hypoalbuminaemia causes bilateral oedema. A post-phlebitic limb usually causes unilateral oedema. However, there are commonly scattered varicosities, lower leg skin pigmentation and probably ulcers. Besides, the exaggerated skin creases favour lymphatic over a venous cause. Arteriovenous malformations cause local gigantism but is accompanied by varicose veins and cutaneous haemangiomas. As the swelling affects the leg and the foot, a tumour is a remote diagnosis.

### Q811: A 22-year-old man. The condition has been present since birth. There is abnormality detected in the groins. The first step in management is

- **A)** Regular observation
- **B)** Ultrasound examination

- **E)** Right orchidectomy

**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The right scrotal compartment is empty since birth. The highest possibility is a neglected right undescended testis, otherwise called incomplete descent of the testis. Rare conditions include agenesis (nonformation) of the testis and ectopic testis, where it lies in a pubic, femoral or perineal position. Retractile testis is seen in infants and young children. In this condition, the scrotal compartment is well-formed. Since the testis is not felt in the groin, an undescended testis may be either in the inguinal canal or intra-abdominal, close to the internal ring. Ultrasound should be the first step. It confirms the presence of a testis and identifies its position.

### Q814: A persistent ulcer with an indurated, everted edge has been present for three months. What is the most likely diagnosis?

- **C)** Squamous cell carcinoma
- **D)** Basal cell carcinoma

**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Squamous cell carcinoma is the most probable diagnosis. You have to check for enlargement of the ipsilateral submandibular and upper deep cervical lymph nodes. A biopsy has to be taken from the edge of the ulcer in order to confirm the diagnosis.

### Q815: An older woman has a distinct palpable breast mass in low-density breast tissue. Mammography shows an irregular infiltrative lesion without a halo. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This is soft-tissue mammography
- **B)** The density of the breast tissue suggests a young woman
- **C)** There is no halo around the lesion
- **D)** The appearance is that of breast cancer
- **E)** Core biopsy under ultrasound guidance is preferred to fine-needle aspiration for a suspicious lesion

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is likely an elderly patient because her breast parenchyma is of low density. This is why the mass is quite distinct. On the other hand, a young breast is commonly dense enough (whitish) to interfere with distinguishing such a lesion. The picture is classic of breast cancer where the lesion has an irregular outline and infiltrates the surrounding tissues. This is in contrast to the benign tumour, fibroadenoma, where a distinct halo surrounds and separates it from the rest of breast tissue. For suspected breast cancer, the trend is to take a core biopsy rather than FNA, and to do it under ultrasound guidance in order to make sure that it samples the most suspicious area.

### Q816: A screening mammogram of an asymptomatic woman shows suspicious breast microcalcifications. The finding is an indication for:

- **A)** Excision of the lesion after wire localization
- **B)** Modified radical mastectomy
- **C)** Neoadjuvant therapy
- **D)** Follow-up
- **E)** None of the above

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Microcalcifications raise the suspicion of duct carcinoma in situ. The lesion is impalpable. A wire is placed through the skin, into the lesion, under mammographic control. The patient is taken to the operating room for excision of the tissues around the tip of the wire. After excision, x-rays are taken of the specimen and of the breast to ensure removal of the suspected area. The excised specimen is sent for histopathological examination.

### Q818: Contrast-enhanced CT shows a well-circumscribed right parotid mass consistent with pleomorphic adenoma. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This is CT with intravenous contrast
- **B)** Intravenous contrast is generally permissible with a serum creatinine of 1. 2 mg/dL
- **C)** Iodinated intravenous contrast is contraindicated in a patient with a relevant contrast allergy
- **D)** The scan shows a well-defined right parotid swelling
- **E)** Treatment is by enucleation of the lesion

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is CT scan with IV contrast as evidenced by the white blood vessels. There are two contraindications for the administration of iodinated IV contrast, renal impairment with elevated serum creatinine (acceptable up to 1. 5 mg/dL) and allergy to iodinated contrast. There is a discrete, apparently well circumscribed swelling in the right parotid gland. The most probable diagnosis is pleomorphic adenoma. Though benign and grossly well circumscribed, in fact, its capsule is defective and it has microscopic extensions in the surrounding gland tissues. Therefore, enucleation is followed by a high incidence of recurrence. The ideal treatment is superficial parotidectomy in order to remove the lesion with a safety margin.

### Q819: A 55-year-old woman has recurrent upper abdominal and chest pain with retching and dyspnoea. What is the diagnosis suggested by her barium meal?

- **C)** Gastric volvulus

**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a typical appearance of gastric volvulus (upside-down stomach). The stomach rotated and moved into the thoracic cavity through a very wide oesophageal hiatus. In fact, gastric volvulus is an advanced stage of paraesophageal hernia. The white arrow points at the cardiac end of the stomach (oesophagogastric junction). The red arrow points at its pyloric end. The yellow arrow points at its greater curvature.

### Q820: A 41-year-old man with a history of alcohol-related pancreatitis has a persistent pancreatic pseudocyst indenting the posterior stomach. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** The CT scan uses intravenous contrast
- **B)** The stomach lies adjacent to the pseudocyst
- **C)** The collection is a pancreatic pseudocyst
- **D)** The kidneys lie posterior to the collection
- **E)** Treatment is excision of the lesion

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The administration of IV contrast is indicated by its presence in the right renal pelvis. It is also indicated by the renal and hepatic brightness. Apparently the patient had an acute attack of acute pancreatitis. It resulted in the formation of this large pancreatic pseudocyst that is seen indenting the back of the stomach. The treatment of such persistent cyst is to drain it into the gastrointestinal tract, by either a cystogastrostomy or a eystojejunostomy. The cyst collapses and gradually disappear. An important point is to warn the patient against alcohol intake and to provide guidance, in order to avoid recurrence of acute pancreatitis and the development of chronic pancreatitis.

- **E)** None of the above

**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`

This is CT scan where the patient has received oral contrast as evidenced by its presence in the stomach (yellow star), and IV contrast as evidenced by by its presence in the aorta (yellow arrow) and the inferior vena cava (blue arrow). 1¥V contrast is contraindicated in patients with impaired renal function as it further worsens it. A serum creatinine of Img/dL is within the normal range of 0. 5-1. 5mg/dL. The red arrows point at focal liver lesions that are not enhancing, i-c. , did not take the IV contrast because of their poor vascularity. Given the clinical picture, multiple non-enhancing focal lesions are highly indicative of metastases.

- **B)** There is a stricture of the lower end of the common bile duct

- **D)** The bile ducts are dilated
- **E)** It is safe to remove the T-tube

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a T-tube cholangiogram that shows normal caliber of bile ducts, no stones and free entry of the contrast in the duodenum. What looks like a stricture of the lower end of common bile duct is the normal smooth tapering that is caused by its passage through the duodenal wall and being surrounded by the sphincter of Oddi. The patient fulfills the prerequisites for safe T-tube removal, which are 1. No filling defects (no stones) in the bile ducts. 2, Free entry of the contrast in the duodenum,

### Q823: A patient undergoes ERCP with biliary duct cannulation and contrast injection. One statement is true:

- **A)** This is MRCP
- **B)** There are many stones in the gallbladder
- **C)** ERCP is a strictly diagnostic intervention
- **D)** Serum amylase may be elevated after this procedure
- **E)** ERCP has no therapeutic role

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is ERCP. The single arrow points at the endoscope. The double arrows point at the common bile duct. The bile duct is dilated. Normally its diameter does not exceed 6mm in a young adult and 8mm in an elderly. Here its diameter is larger than that of the endoscope, which is about 10mm. It contains many stones that show as filling defects. At this procedure the operator would divide the duodenal papilla and the sphincter of Oddi, then remove the stones using a balloon catheter or a basket. Possible complications of ERCP include bleeding, duodenal perforation, cholangitis and pancreatitis. The latter markedly raises serum amylase and lipase.

### Q824: Abdominal ultrasound demonstrates echogenic gallstones with posterior acoustic shadows. All the following statements are true except:

- **D)** The posterior acoustic shadows identify the common bile duct

**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The yellow arrows point at the posterior echogenic shadow of the stones. Stones in the gallbladder are diagnosed on ultrasound on the basis of being echogenic (white) and having a posterior echo shadow (black). The majority of gallstones are silent. They cause no symptoms and may be discovered on ultrasound or CT examination for another condition. In general, these are not indicated for surgery.

### Q827: An occlusal radiograph of the mandible shows a radiopaque stone in the left submandibular gland or duct. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This is a panoramic view of the mandible
- **B)** Fasting is not needed for this study
- **C)** It shows a left submandibular stone
- **D)** The lesion may cause intermittent postprandial submandibular swelling
- **E)** Treatment of the lesion is surgical

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** In fact, this is an occlusal view of the mandible. The shown radiopaque shadow is a stone in the left submandibular gland or its duct. It usually presents by the appearance of a postprandial submandibular swelling that resolves gradually, until it reappears with the following meal. Obstruction may be complicated by infection, where the condition turns into acute sialadenitis. Treatment is surgical. If the stone is in the duct and is palpable through the floor of the mouth, its removal is done transorally. If it is not palpable, i. e. , resides in the gland, treatment is excision of the whole gland. Submandibular sialoadenectomy for stone disease is difficult because of the dense adhesions that are caused by the chronic inflammation.

### Q832: A 79-year-old male complains of poor appetite, weight loss and progressive weakness for the last three months. For the last week he had about two bouts of vomiting each day. His haemoglobin level is 7. 6g/dL. The following statements are all true, except

- **A)** The most probable diagnosis is benign pyloric obstruction
- **B)** Placement of a nasogastric tube is indicated
- **C)** IV fluid and electrolyte replacement is indicated
- **D)** Upper GI endoscopy is indicated
- **E)** The stomach is markedly distended

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Poor appetite, weight loss, anaemia and weakness, particularly when progressive in an elderly person are highly suspicious of visceral malignancy. The usual sites are the liver, stomach, body and tail of pancreas and right colon. Vomiting, plus the large size of the stomach on barium meal examination suggest malignancy of the distal part. The stomach should be emptied with a nasogastric tube for a few days. Meanwhile, water, electrolyte and nutritional deficiencies are corrected intravenously. Upper GI endoscopy and biopsy of the stenosed segment are then done to settle the diagnosis.

### Q834: A barium enema shows an irregular narrowing in the sigmoid colon. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This is a barium enema
- **B)** The usual complaint is progressive constipation
- **C)** The lesion is in the sigmoid colon
- **D)** The rest of the colon should be visualized
- **E)** The patient necessarily has acute intestinal obstruction

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a case of sigmoid cancer as shown by the irregular narrowing. However, confirmation of its nature by colonoscopy and biopsy is essential before starting treatment. Colonoscopy has another value, which is visualization of the rest of the colon because 4-5% of colorectal cancers are multiple. Cancer of the left colon classically presents with progressive constipation. Sometimes, the patient also complains of attacks of mucus diarrhoea and sometimes with bleeding per rectum. The patient may be having chronic, but not acute obstruction. Plain Xray rather than barium enema is the indicated imaging study in acute cases. Furthermore, there is no dilatation proximal to the lesion.

### Q835: Plain erect and supine abdominal X-rays show small-bowel gaseous distension consistent with small-bowel obstruction. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** The right-hand X-ray is erect and the patient is standing
- **B)** The expected lesion is cancer of the splenic flexure
- **C)** Examination of the groin is essential
- **D)** Abdominal tenderness and polymorphonuclear leucocytosis may indicate the need for urgent surgery
- **E)** Placement of a nasogastric tube is indicated

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** These are plain X-rays of the abdomen. The one to the right is erect (patient is standing), while the one to the left is supine (patient is lying down). They show small intestinal gaseous distension (the small intestine normally contains no gas). This is more evident on the supine film (left). Most of the distended loops are jejunal as obvious by the mucosal folds that are called valvulae conniventes. They give the distended jejunal loops a concertina appearance. The colon, which normally contains gas is not shown (empty). This is enough to diagnose small intestinal obstruction. The commonest cause of acute small intestinal obstruction is postoperative adhesions. The next common is strangulated hernia. In fact, this particular patient turned out to have a strangulated femoral hernia, which has been misdiagnosed as femoral lymphadenitis. Examination of all hernia orifices is an essential component of evaluating these patients.

---

### Q836: Abdominal imaging shows marked colonic dilatation from the caecum to the descending colon with an abrupt transition and no small-bowel distension. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** The radiological diagnosis is colonic obstruction
- **B)** The patient usually presents early with vomiting and colicky pain
- **C)** The caecum is the part of bowel that suffers the greatest dilatation
- **D)** Perforation is more likely if the ileocaecal valve is competent
- **E)** Carcinoma is a common cause of this appearance

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The patient has marked gaseous distension which extends from the caecum to the descending colon, where it abruptly terminates. There is no small intestinal distension. This is a picture of acute colon obstruction, the most probable cause of which is carcinoma of the left colon. The earliest manifestations are colicky pain, marked abdominal distension and absolute constipation. Vomiting is a late symptom in cases of colon obstruction. In this patient the ileocaecal valve is competent as evident by the absence of gas in the ileum. This situation is called “closed loop obstruction” The distended colon segment is closed on both sides. It results in marked rise of pressure in this segment. The caecum suffers the maximum distension and is, therefore, the expected site of perforation if the case is neglected.

---

### Q837: An invertogram in a neonate with an anorectal anomaly shows distal bowel gas above the pelvic floor, defined by the line from coccyx to pubic symphysis. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** The line represents the pelvic floor
- **B)** This is a low anorectal anomaly
- **C)** The colon is distended
- **D)** A colon pull-through operation is not needed
- **E)** A rectovesical fistula is expected in this case

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is an invertogram where the baby is held upside-down for a while before taking the shot. This allows gas in the rectum to settle as distal as possible. The dotted line joins the tip of coccyx with the symphysis pubis. It, thus, represents the pelvic floor. Gas in the rectum is seen to cross this dotted line. The case is, therefore, a low rectal anomaly. Treatment is by a minor perineal operation. A rectovesical or vaginal fistula accompanies high anomalies.

### Q838: A 37-year-old man has blood-stained mucus per rectum. Barium enema demonstrates multiple colonic polyps. Choose the one correct statement:

- **A)** This X-ray belongs to a child
- **B)** This is a double-contrast barium enema
- **C)** The findings are multiple diverticula
- **D)** Colonoscopy is indicated
- **E)** The descending colon shows a stricture

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This X-ray belongs to an adult as evidenced by fusion of the acetabulum, which occurs around the age of 20. This is barium enema. There is no gas in the colon. Therefore, it is not a double contrast test. The arrows point at multiple polyps which show here as filling defects. Diverticula appear as outpoutchings from the colon. Colonoscopy is certainly indicated in order to examine the whole colon and to biopsy or do polypectomy of the most suspicious lesions. If these turn out to be adenomatous polyps, total proctocolectomy is indicated as the condition is precancerous. The family members should be regularly examined because adenomatous polyposis coli is an autosomal dominant disease. The area that looks like a stricture in the descending colon is, in fact, a peristaltic contraction. To diagnose a stricture, it should be a constant feature on multiple films, but more importantly there should be some proximal dilatation.

### Q840: CT with intravenous contrast in a jaundiced woman shows a pancreatic-head mass (white arrow), a distended gallbladder (yellow arrow), and the inferior vena cava (red arrow). All the following statements are true except:

- **B)** The white arrow points at a thick-walled gallbladder
- **C)** The red arrow points at the inferior vena cava
- **D)** Alkaline phosphatase is expected to be elevated

**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The administration of 1V contrast is known by the white colour of the aorta, inferior vena cava (red arrow) and renal parenchyma. Two precautions are important before 1V administration of a contrast 1. Exclusion of iodine sensitivity by history taking, and taking precautions by keeping hydrocortisone, adrenaline and antihistamines ready at hand when administering it. This risk has been greatly reduced by the introduction of non-iodinated contrast materials. 2. Ensuring normal renal function. Such contrast material further causes deterioration of renal function. A normal serum creatinine is a prerequisite. The white arrow points at a mass in the head of pancreas. The mass is not enhancing with the contrast (hypodense). This, together with the history and a distended gallbladder (yellow arrow) are features of cancer of the head of pancreas. Alkaline phosphatase and GGT are elevated in cases of obstructive jaundice.

### Q842: A 70-year-old man with accidental discovery of a painless upper abdominal swelling. All the following statements are true, except.

### Q844: A 52-year-old woman with diabetes and obesity has gas-forming infection in the first web space and osteomyelitis of the fourth metatarsal. All the following statements are true except:

- **C)** Amputation of toes in this patient proves the presence of ischaemia

**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This patient has diabetic foot infection as evidenced by the gas-forming infection in the first web space (gas bubbles) and by the osteomyelitis of the 4" metatarsal. Though ischaemia might be present, amputation was most probably done because of infection. Amputation, in this case, can be done in a well-vascularized foot. A neuropathic ulcer is commonly found on the plantar surface because of the blunted sensation of neuropathy. The usual site is opposite the head of the first metatarsal bone (weight-bearing point). It provides access to deep infection.

### Q845: A patient has a Colles fracture of the distal radius. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** Osteoporosis is a frequent predisposing factor
- **B)** The fracture can result from a relatively minor fall
- **C)** The distal radial fragment is usually displaced and angulated laterally
- **D)** Open reduction and internal fixation is the rule
- **E)** Wrist stiffness is a possible complication

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is fracture of the distal radius, which is known as Colles’ fracture, otherwise also known as old-woman’s fracture. The reason behind this name is the prevalence of postmenopausal osteoporosis, which allows a minor trauma as breaking a fall by the outstretched hand to fracture the bone. The usual treatment is closed reduction and fixation in a below-elbow plaster cast.

### Q846: An elderly patient with an osteoporotic fracture of the femoral neck has an extracapsular fracture. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This fracture is common in the elderly
- **B)** The fracture can result from minor trauma
- **C)** This fracture is commonly compound
- **D)** The fracture is extracapsular
- **E)** The usual treatment is internal fixation

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is fracture of the neck of femur, which is a common fracture in the elderly who have osteoporosis. The usual trauma is a minor one. The fracture is extracapsular as it is distal to the line that connects the greater and lesser trochanters. Fractures of the femoral neck are almost always closed because the bone is surrounded by thick muscles. The usual treatment is by internal fixation using a dynamic hip screw. The operation allows early ambulation in order to prevent complications of prolonged recumbency, particularly at this advanced age.

### Q847: An elderly patient has a markedly displaced intracapsular femoral neck fracture with interruption of the blood supply to the femoral head. All the following statements are true except:

- **B)** The fracture can result from minor trauma
- **C)** The fracture is intracapsular
- **D)** Necrosis of the femoral head is expected
- **E)** The usual treatment is internal fixation

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Another case of femoral neck fracture. Here the fracture is intracapsular (proximal to the intertrochanteric line) and is markedly displaced. These two features indicate interruption to the blood supply of the femoral head and, hence, necrosis is expected. Treatment is by hemiarthroplasty, i. e. , replacement of the head of femur with a prosthesis.

### Q848: A child has bilateral posterior hip dislocation (developmental dysplasia of the hip). All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** A familial tendency is often present
- **B)** The lesion is bilateral
- **C)** The child has posterior hip dislocation
- **D)** Ultrasound is an effective method of diagnosis
- **E)** Treatment is essentially surgical

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This child has bilateral posterior hip dislocation. The condition is also known as developmental dysplasia of the hip. Ultrasound is becoming part of the routine assessment of all neonates. It can detect the position of the femoral head with reasonable accuracy. Xray examination is of little value in the newborn because the head of the femur is entirely cartilaginous and not visible radiologically. Treatment is usually conservative by using a splint that keeps the hip of the neonate or infant in the acetabulum long enough to ensure stability. Surgery may be needed in neglected cases.

### Q849: CT shows a right renal cell carcinoma (hypernephroma). All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This tumour is usually multicentric
- **B)** This tumour may be painless
- **C)** This tumour may spread by blood and lymphatics
- **D)** Treatment is mainly surgical
- **E)** This tumour may cause polycythaemia

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a right renal cell carcinoma (hypernephroma). The urinary neoplasm that is multicentric is the transitional cell carcinoma that arises from the lining epithelium of the renal peives, ureters and urinary bladder. Painless intermittent total haematuria is the standard description of hypernephroma symptoms. However, pain is not rare and may occur because of stretch of the renal capsule and because of blood clots that descend down the ureter. Blood loss causes anaemia, but paradoxically, in rare cases, the tumour secretes excess erythropoietin and causes polythycaemia.

### Q850: A child has an extension-type supracondylar fracture of the humerus. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This is a greenstick fracture
- **B)** The fracture can injure the ulnar nerve
- **C)** The fracture can injure the brachial artery
- **D)** The fracture is of the extension type
- **E)** Treatment is essentially closed reduction and immobilization

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a supracondylar fracture of the humerus, which is a common injury, particularly in children. It is of the extension type, which is more frequent than the flexion variety. This is known by the position of the distal fragment, which is displaced posteriorly, i-e. , extended. As the posterior cortex is intact, the fracture is described as green stick. The sharp anterior edge of the proximal fragment may injure the brachial artery and/or the median nerve. The ulnar nerve is not at danger with this fracture as it runs posterior to the humerus, more precisely posterior to the medial epicondyle. Treatment is essentially conservative by closed reduction and fixation in a plaster cast. Surgery is needed when there is vascular or neural injury.

### Q853: An elderly patient has a chronic subdural haematoma with a concavo-convex, hypodense appearance after minor head trauma. All the following statements are true except:

The condition is commonly caused by minor trauma as bumping the head on a doorway or while entering a car. The small brain of an elderly moves within the wider skull. The subdural veins are exposed to shearing movement which tears them.

### Q855: A severely injured patient has respiratory distress, multiple right rib fractures, and subcutaneous emphysema. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** There are multiple rib fractures on the right side

- **D)** The subcutaneous emphysema is a right pneumothorax
- **E)** This trauma may have injured the liver

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The arrow points at air outside the chest cavity. Subcutaneous air is called surgical emphysema. Multiple rib fractures indicate severe trauma. If multiple ribs are fractured, each at more than one point, a flail segment results. This causes lung contusion and reduces the efficiency of respiration.

### Q856: IVP shows a low-lying left kidney, right hydronephrosis, and a smooth filling defect at the bladder base consistent with benign prostatic hyperplasia. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** This is an intravenous pyelogram
- **B)** The left kidney is ptosed
- **C)** There is right hydronephrosis
- **D)** There is an osteolytic lesion in the right iliac bone
- **E)** There is a smooth filling defect at the base of the bladder

**Correct Answer:** D
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is an intravenous pyelography (IVP), otherwise known as intravenous urography (IVU) as it shows the whole urinary tract. The left kidney is at a lower position than normal. Naturally, the pelviureteric junction lies opposite L2. Here it lies opposite L3. Furthermore, the left kidney, which normally lies higher than the right one, is here at a lower level. The right side calyces are ballooned, indicating hydronephrosis. Gas in the right colon overlies the right iliac bone and gives a false impression of a large lytic lesion. However, the blackness of colonic gas is seen to extend above the iliac crest, up to overlap the lower part of the kidney. The smooth filling defect at the base of the bladder suggests a bilobar benign prostatic hyperplasia.

### Q858: A road-traffic-accident victim has dyspnoea and a sizable traumatic left haemothorax. Which listed next step is correct?

- **D)** All of the above

**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a sizable traumatic left haemothorax as indicated by the opacity that obliterates the costophrenic angle and is rising towards the axilla. The condition requires the placement of a chest tube in the left 5‘ midaxillary line in order to drain the blood and to allow lung inflation. In the majority of cases, lung inflation compresses the bleeding vessels, and stops bleeding.

### Q861: A bone isotope scan is performed to look for metastases. Which primary cancer is least likely to cause bone metastases?

- **A)** Colorectal cancer
- **B)** Breast cancer
- **C)** Prostate cancer
- **D)** Lung cancer
- **E)** Renal cell carcinoma

**Correct Answer:** A
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is an isotope scan of bones that is requested to look for bony metastases. Venous drainage of colorectal cancer is through the portal circulation. Metastases, therefore, lodge in the liver. Bone deposits are quite uncommon with colorectal cancer. Venous drainage of the other cancers is systemic. Metastases affect red bone marrow, rather than the yellow. Red marrow is generally present in the central skeleton (skull, ribs, spine, pelvis and proximal humerus and femur).

### Q862: A patient with right tension pneumothorax is dyspnoeic and in shock. What is the immediate management?

- **A)** Urgent CT scan
- **B)** Needle decompression in the right second intercostal space
- **C)** Transfer to the operating room for urgent right thoracotomy
- **D)** Urgent placement of a chest tube under local anaesthesia
- **E)** Cross-match blood for urgent transfusion

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The patient has right tension pneumothorax. The right lung is completely collapsed. The mediastinum is shifted to the left, partially collapsing the left lung, as well. The patient is dyspneic and in shock, though neck veins are congested. This is an emergency situation that requires immediate release of tension in the right pleural cavity. The fastest method is immediate insertion of wide-bore needies, while preparing for placement of a chest tube. Time should not be wasted in doing imaging studies. In fact, this chest X-ray should not have been done in the first place. Tension pneumothorax is diagnosed clinically with confidence.

### Q864: IVP in a 39-year-old man with recurrent left loin pain shows left hydronephrosis caused by a small upper ureteric stone; the right kidney is normal. All the following statements are true except:

- **A)** There is left hydronephrosis
- **B)** The left kidney shows fair function
- **C)** The right kidney shows early hydronephrosis
- **D)** The radiopaque shadow is most probably a stone in the left ureter
- **E)** The rest of the urinary tract should be assessed for distal obstruction

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** The calyces of the left kidney are ballooned, indicating hydronephrosis. Though hydronephrotic, this kidney shows good excretion and concentration of the contrast, indicating fair function. The right kidney maintains the normal cupping of calyces, as well as the normal shape and size of its pelvis. This is a normal kidney as far as morphology and function are concerned. The radio-opaque shadow lies along the course of the upper left ureter, which descends over the transverse processes of L2 to L5. It is actually a ureter stone and is the cause of left hydronephrosis. We certainly need to see the rest of the urinary tract in order to check for distal obstruction. The stone is less than Smm. In the presence of this good kidney function, absence of distal ureter obstruction allows conservative treatment to be successful. The stone can be passed without intervention.

---

### Q865: An adult IVP shows a normal urinary tract and an irregular bladder filling defect suspicious for malignancy. All the following statements are true except:

- **B)** The shape of the bladder filling defect suggests malignancy
- **C)** The kidneys show back-pressure changes
- **D)** The next step is cystoscopic biopsy
- **E)** Both kidneys are in normal position

**Correct Answer:** C
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is an adult because of the complete union of the acetabular cartilage. Absolutely normal kidneys. Normal excretion and concentration of the contrast material. Normal shape of the kidneys and ureters. An irregular filling defect in the urinary bladder is suggestive of a malignant neoplasm. The common types are transitional cell carcinoma and squamous cell carcinoma. Cystoscopy and biopsy are needed to document the diagnosis. Both kidneys are in normal position; the pelviureteric junctions are opposite L2, the right kidney is a little lower than the left.

### Q866: A 17-year-old boy sustains a perineal kick, develops severe pain and blood at the urethral meatus, and has an ascending urethrogram showing contrast leakage from the bulbar urethra while the posterior urethra remains intact. One statement is true:

- **A)** This is micturition urethrography
- **B)** There is injury to the bulbar part of the urethra
- **C)** The urethral injury is complete
- **D)** The patient is encouraged to void to test urethral continuity
- **E)** This injury is commonly associated with pelvic fractures

**Correct Answer:** B
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** Trauma to the perineum is the cause of injury to the bulbar urethra. Here the posterior urethra is intact, while the contrast leaks from the proximal part of the anterior urethra. This is ascending urethrography where a catheter is placed in the distal urethra (as seen here) and the contrast is injected. The injury is incomplete because part of the contrast reached up to the posterior urethra and the bladder. The patient is instructed not to void in order to avoid urine extravasation in the subcutaneous tissues. The safest management is to place a suprapubic cystostomy tube to divert urine and allow the injury to heal. If a stricture results, treatment is by dilatation. Fractures of the pelvis are caused by severe trauma as road traffic accidents and falls from a height. These are associated with injuries of the posterior urethra and/or extraperitoneal rupture of the bladder.

### Q868: A woman is struck by a car and develops knee pain, an expanding popliteal fossa haematoma, and absent pedal pulses on the side of a supracondylar femoral fracture. What is the best treatment for the fracture?

- **A)** Skin traction
- **B)** Skeletal traction
- **C)** Closed reduction under general anaesthesia and plaster cast
- **D)** External metal fixation
- **E)** Internal fixation

**Correct Answer:** E
**Type:** QCS
**Answer Source:** key
**Year:** None
**Tag:** `External, Dr Amr Mohsen`
**Explanation:** This is a supracondylar fracture of the femur. This fracture is infamous for injuring the popliteal artery. Notice the jagged edge of the distal fracture fragment. The clinical picture points at popliteal artery injury. Open reduction and internal fixation is the answer in order to repair the artery and to rigidly fix the fracture to prevent any shearing movement that may re-injure it.
